ValidationError> {\n   120→    if let Some(page) = v {\n   121→        if *page == 0 {\n   122→            return Err(ValidationError::new(\"page_must_be_positive\"));\n   123→        }\n   124→     }\n   144→    }\n   145→}\n   146→\n   147→////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n   148→//  PAGINATION UTILITIES   let result: Option<Vec<u8>> =\n   396→            timeout(Duration::from_millis(30), conn.get(key)).await??;\n   397→        Ok(result)\n   398→    }\n   399→\n   400→    async fn persist_cache<T: /////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n\nconst MAX_PER_PAGE: u16 = 250;\n\n#[derive(Debug, Deserialize, Validate)]\npub struct ListQuery {\n    #[validate(custom = \"validate_page\")]\n    pub page: Option<u32>,\n\n    #[validate(custom = \"validate_per_page\")]\n    pub per_page: Option<u16>,\n\n    /// Optional filter on ledger account\n    #[serde(default)]\n    #[validate(length(min = 1, max = 30))]\n    pub account_code: Option<String>,\n}\n\nfn validate_page(v: &Option<u32>) -> Result<(), ValidationError> {\n    if let Some(page) = v {\n        if *page == 0 {\n            return Err(ValidationError::new(\"page_must_be_positive\"));\n        }\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n\nfn validate_per_page(v: &Option<u16>) -> Result<(), ValidationError> {\n    if let Some(per_page) = v {\n        if *per_page == 0 || *per_page > MAX_PER_PAGE {\n            return Err(ValidationError::new(\"per_page_out_of_range\"));\n        }\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n\nimpl Default for ListQuery {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            page: Some(1),\n            per_page: Some(50),\n            account_code: None,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n//  PAGINATION UTILITIES ///////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize)]\npub struct Paginated<T> {\n    pub data: Vec<T>,\n    pub page: u32,\n    pub per_page: u16,\n    pub total_items: u64,\n    pub total_pages: u32,\n}\n\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n//  AUTHENTICATION MIDDLEWARE //////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct JwtAuth {\n    pub tenant_id: Uuid,\n    pub user_id: Uuid,\n}\n\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum AuthError {\n    #[error(\"Unauthorized\")]\n    Unauthorized,\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl<S> FromRequestParts<S> for JwtAuth\nwhere\n    S: Send + Sync,\n{\n    type Rejection = ApiError;\n\n    async fn from_request_parts(parts: &mut Parts, _state: &S) -> Result<Self, Self::Rejection> {\n        let TypedHeader(Authorization(bearer)) =\n            TypedHeader::<Authorization<Bearer>>::from_request_parts(parts, _state)\n                .await\n                .map_err(|_| \"tenant:<tenant_id>:user:<user_id>\"\n        let mut iter = token_str.split(':');\n        let prefix = iter.next().unwrap_or_default();\n        if prefix != \"tenant\" {\n            return Err(ApiError::unauthorized());\n        }\n        let tenant_id = iter\n            .next()\n            .and_then(|s| Uuid::parse_str(s).ok())\n            .ok_or_else(ApiError::unauthorized)?;\n\n /////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n\n#[async_trait]\npub ////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n\npub struct PgLedgerEntryRepo {\n    pool: PgPool,\n}\n\nimpl PgLedgerEntryRepo {\n    pub fn new(pool: PgPool) -> Self {\n        Self { pool }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl LedgerEntryRepository for PgLedgerEntryRepo {\n    #[instrument(skip(self, query))]\n    async fn list(\n        &self,\n        tenant_id: Uuid,\n        query: &ListQuery,\n    ) -> Result<(Vec<LedgerEntry>, u64), RepositoryError> {\n        let page = query.page.unwrap_or(1);\n        let per_page = query.per_page.unwrap_or(50) as i64;\n        let offset = ((page - 1) as i64) * per_page;\n\n        // Filtering\n        let (entries, total): (Vec<LedgerEntry>, i64) = if let Some(ref account) = query.account_code\n        {\n            let rows = sqlx::query_as::<_, LedgerEntry>(\n                r#\"\n                SELECT *\n                FROM ledger_entries\n                WHERE tenant_id = $1\n                  AND account_code = $2\n                ORDER BY occurred_at DESC\n                OFFSET $3\n                LIMIT $4\n                \"#,\n            )\n            .bind(tenant_id)\n            .bind(account)\n            .bind(offset)\n            .bind(per_page)\n            .fetch_all(&self.pool)\n            .await?;\n\n            let total: (i64,) = sqlx::query_as(\n                r#\"\n                SELECT COUNT(*) as count\n                FROM ledger_entries\n                WHERE tenant_id = $1\n                  AND account_code = $2\n                \"#,\n            )\n            .bind(tenant_id)\n            .bind(account)\n            .fetch_one(&self.pool)\n            .await?;\n\n            (rows, total.0)\n        } else {\n            let rows = sqlx::query_as::<_, LedgerEntry>(\n                r#\"\n                SELECT *\n                FROM ledger_entries\n                WHERE tenant_id = $1\n                ORDER BY occurred_at DESC\n                OFFSET $2\n                LIMIT $3\n                \"#,\n            )\n            .bind(tenant_id)\n            .bind(offset)\n            .bind(per_page)\n            .fetch_all(&self.pool)\n            .await?;\n\n            let total: (i64,) = sqlx::query_as(\n                r#\"\n                SELECT COUNT(*) as count\n                FROM ledger_entries\n                WHERE tenant_id = $1\n                \"#,\n            )\n            .bind(tenant_id)\n            .fetch_one(&self.pool)\n            .await?;\n\n            (rows, total.0)\n        };\n\n        Ok((entries, total as u64))\n LedgerEntryQuerySvc<R> {\n    repo: Arc<R>,\n    redis: RedisPool,\n}\n\nimpl<R> LedgerEntryQuerySvc<R>\nwhere\n    R: LedgerEntryRepository + 'static,\n{\n    pub fn new(repo: Arc<R>, redis: #[instrument(skip(self))]\n    pub async fn list(\n        &self,\n        tenant: Uuid,\n        query: ListQuery,\n    ) -> Result<Paginated<LedgerEntryDto>, ServiceError> {\n        // 1. Try cache (30ms timeout to avoid head-of-line blocking)\n        let key = Self::cache_key(tenant, &query);\n        if let Ok(Some(raw)) = self.try_fetch_cache(&key).await {\n            debug!(%tenant, %key, \"Cache hit\");\n            return serde_json::from_slice::<Paginated<LedgerEntryDto>>(&raw)\n                .map_err(ServiceError::CacheSerialization);\n        }\n\n        // 2. Query repository\n        debug!(%tenant, \"Cache miss – querying DB\");\n        let (entries, total) = self.repo.list(tenant, &query).await?;\n        let dto: Vec<LedgerEntryDto> = entries.into_iter().map(Into::into).collect();\n\n        let page = query.page.unwrap_or(1);\n        let per_page = query.per_page.unwrap_or(50);\n        let total_pages = ((total as f64) / (per_page as f64)).ceil() as u32;\n\n        let payload = Paginated {\n            data: dto,\n            page,\n            per_page,\n            total_items: total,\n            total_pages,\n        };\n\n        // 3. Backfill cache (best-effort)\n        let _ = self.persist_cache(&key, &payload).await;\n\n        Ok(payload)\n    }\n\n    async fn try_fetch_cache(&self, key: &str) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, ServiceError> {\n        let mut conn: RedisConn = self.redis.get().await?;\n        // short timeout to protect against redis hiccups\n        let result: Option<Vec<u8>> =\n            timeout(Duration::from_millis(30), conn.get(key)).await??;\n        Ok(result)\n    }\n\n    async fn persist_cache<T: Serialize>(&self, key: &str, value: &T) -> Result<(), ServiceError> {\n        let mut conn: RedisConn = self.redis.get().await?;\n        let data = serde_json::to_vec(value)?;\n        let _: () = conn\n            .set_ex(key, data, 60) // 60 seconds TTL\n            .await\n            .map_err(ServiceError::Cache)?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum ServiceError {\n    #[error(\"repository error: {0}\")]\n    Repo(#[from] &self,\n        query: &LedgerEntryQuery,\n    ) -> Result<(Vec<LedgerEntryView>, u64), ServiceError> {\n        let timer = Instant::now();\n\n        // Build dynamic filter predicates.\n        let mut conditions: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();\n        let mut args: Vec<Box<dyn sqlx::Encode<'_, sqlx::Postgres> + Send + Sync>> = Vec::new();\n\n        if let Some(acc_id) = query.account_id {\n            conditions.push(\"account_id = $1\");\n            args.push(Box::new(acc_id));\n        }\n        if let Some(from) = query.from_date {\n            conditions.push(\"posted_at >= $2\");\n            args.push(Box::new(from));\n        }\n        if let Some(to) = query.to_date {\n            conditions.push(\"posted_at <= $3\");\n            args.push(Box::new(to.succ_opt().unwrap_or(to))); // };\n\n        // Count total rows for pagination meta.\n        let count_stmt = format!(\"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM ledger_entries {}\", where_clause);\n        let total: (i64,) = sqlx::query_as_with(&count_stmt, args.clone())\n            .fetch_one(&self.pool)\n            .await\n            .map_err(ServiceError::Repository)?;\n\n        // Main SELECT page.\n        let limit = query.limit() as i64;\n        let offset = query.offset() as i64;\n        let select_stmt = format!(\n            \"SELECT id, account_id, amount, currency, posted_at, memo \\\n             FROM ledger_entries {} \\\n             ORDER BY posted_at DESC \\\n             LIMIT $4 OFFSET $5\",\n            where_clause\n        );\n\n        // Append pagination args\n        args.push(Box::new(limit));\n        args.push(Box::new(offset));\n\n        // We need to transmute Box<dyn ...> to actual values; easiest path is to\n        // re-build query with `sqlx::query` binding in sequence.\n        let mut q = sqlx::query(&select_stmt);\n        for (idx, arg) in args.into_iter().enumerate() {\n            q = q.bind(arg); // SAFE: sqlx uses runtime type checking\n .into_iter()\n            .map(|row| LedgerEntryView {\n                id: row.get(\"id\"),\n                account_id: row.get(\"account_id\"),\n                amount: row.get(\"amount\"),\n _>(\"memo\").map(ToOwned::to_owned),\n            })\n            .collect();\n\n        debug!(\n            took_ms = timer.elapsed().as_millis() as u64,\n            \"LedgerEntryRepository::fetch_page executed\"\n        );\n\n        Ok((entries, total.0 as u64))\n    }\n}\n\n/* ---------- Cache provider  ---------- */\n\n#[async_trait]\npub trait CacheProvider: async fn get_cached_page(\n        &self,\n        cache_key: &str,\n    ) -> Result<Option<Paginated<LedgerEntryView>>, ServiceError> {\n        let mut conn = self.client.get_async_connection().await?;\n        let data: Option<String> = conn.get(cache_key).await?;\n        match data {\n            Some(raw) => {\n                let page: Paginated<LedgerEntryView> =\n                    serde_json::from_str(&raw).map_err(|e| ServiceError::Unknown(e.to_string()))?;\n                Ok(Some(page))\n            }\n            None => conn.set_ex(cache_key, serialized, CACHE_TTL_SECONDS)\n            .await\n            .map_err(ServiceError::Cache)\n    }\n}\n\n/* ---------- Service layer  ---------- */\n\npub struct Cash-Flow Forecast query service with optional Redis-backed response\n//! caching, pagination helpers and domain-driven abstractions.\n//!\n//! This module is completely self-contained yet showcases the LedgerLink\n//! Nexus architecture principles:\n//!   • Clean separation between View (DTO)  ➜  View-Model (validated\n//!     transport structs)  ➜  Domain Models / Repository  ➜  Service.\n//!   • Transparent response-caching with Redis using a typed cache key.\n//!   • Structured error handling with `thiserror` and `tracing`.\n//!   • Feature-gated compile options (`response_caching`) for optional\n//!     dependencies, keeping binary size and attack-surface minimal.\n//!\n//! Down-stream HTTP/GraphQL handlers can simply call\n//! `CashFlowForecastService::get_forecast(..)` and receive a fully\n//! validated, paginated & (optionally) cached payload ready to be\n//! serialized by the transport layer.\n\n#![allow(clippy::async_yields_async)] // False-positive for async_trait\n#![cfg_attr(docsrs, feature(doc_cfg))]\n\nuse std::{num::NonZeroU32, sync::Arc, NaiveDate,\n    pub to: NaiveDate,\n}\n\nimpl DateRange {\n    pub fn validate(self) -> Result<Self, ValidationError> {\n        if self.from > self.to {\n            Err(ValidationError::InvalidDateRange)\n        } else {\n            Ok(self)\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Simple pagination descriptor (page & per_page are both 1-based).\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct Pagination {\n    page: NonZeroU32,\n    per_page: NonZeroU32,\n}\n\nimpl Pagination {\n    pub fn new(page: u32, per_page: u32) -> Result<Self, ValidationError> {\n        let page = NonZeroU32::new(page).ok_or(ValidationError::InvalidPage)?;\n        let per_page =\n            NonZeroU32::new(per_page).ok_or(ValidationError::InvalidPageSize)?;\n        Ok(Self { page, per_page })\n    }\n\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn offset(&self) -> u32 {\n        (self.page.get() - 1) * self.per_page.get()\n    }\n\n    #[inline]\n    pub fn limit(&self) -> u32 {\n        self.per_page.get()\n    }\n}\n\n/// Domain aggregate representing a single cash-flow forecast entry.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct CashFlowForecast {\n    pub id: Uuid,\n    pub account_id: Uuid,\n    pub date: NaiveDate,\n    pub expected_inflow: Decimal,\n    pub expected_outflow: Uuid,\n    pub date: NaiveDate,\n    pub expected_inflow: Decimal,\n    pub expected_outflow: Decimal,\n    pub currency: String,\n}\n\nimpl From<CashFlowForecast> for CashFlowForecastDto {\n    fn f.expected_outflow,\n            currency: f.currency,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Output envelope with pagination metadata.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize)]\npub struct CashFlowForecastPage {\n    pub items: Vec<CashFlowForecastDto>,\n    pub total_items: u64,\n    pub page: u32,\n    pub per_page: u32,\n}\n\n/// Domain-driven repository abstraction.\n///\n/// Implementations live in separate #[cfg(feature = \"response_caching\")]\n    ttl: Duration,\n}\n\nimpl<R, C> CashFlowForecastService<R, C>\nwhere\n    R: CashFlowForecastRepository,\n{\n    pub fn new(repo: Arc<R>) -> Self\n    where\n        C: Default,\n    {\n        Self {\n            repo,\n            #[cfg(feature = \"response_caching\")]\n            cache: Arc::<C>::default(),\n            #[cfg(feature = .map_err(ServiceError::Repository)?;\n        debug!(\"db_query completed in {:?}\", started_at.elapsed());\n\n        let page = CashFlowForecastPage {\n            items: ConnectionLike + Send + Sync + 'static>(pub tokio::sync::Mutex<B>);\n\n#[cfg(all(feature = \"response_caching\", feature = \"redis\"))]\n#[async_trait]\nimpl<B> CacheBackend for RedisCache<B>\nwhere\n    B: ConnectionLike + Send + Sync + 'static,\n{\n    async fn get<T>(&self, key: &CacheKey) -> Result<Option<T>, CacheError>\n    where\n        T: for<'de> Deserialize<'de> + Send,\n    {\n        let redis_key = key.as_redis_key();\n        let mut conn = self.0.lock().await;\n        let data: Option<String> = conn.get(&redis_key).await?;\n        Ok(match data {\n            Some(s) => {\n                let parsed = serde_json::from_str::<T>(&s)?;\n                Some(parsed)\n            }\n            None => None,\n        })\n    }\n\n    async fn set<T>(\n        &self,\n        key: CacheKey,\n        value: &T,\n        ttl: Duration,\n    ) -> Result<(), CacheError>\n    where\n        T: Serialize + Send + Sync,\n    {\n        let redis_key = key.as_redis_key();\n       .await?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n//\n// ----------------------------- Errors -----------------------------------\n//\n\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum ValidationError {\n Pagination & Caching\n   335→Nexus employs _cursor-based pagination_ (`page[cursor]`). Cursors are opaque Base64-encoded and signed.\n   336→\n   337→Responses MAY include:\n   338→\n   339→```\n   340→Cache-Control: private, max-age=30, stale-while-revalidate=30\n   341→ETag: W/\"67d32bca9\"\n   342→```\n   343→\n   344→Clients SHOULD provide `If-None-Match` for cache hits and store cursors per filter signature.\n   345→\n   346→---\n   347→\n   348→## 8. Rate Limiting\n   349→Default production limits:\n   350→\n   351→| Resource            | Limit                       |\n   352→|---------------------|-----------------------------|\n   353→| **Authenticated**   | 600 requests / minute / key |\n   354→| **Unauthenticated** | *Not supported*             |\n   355→\n   356→Headers:\n   357→\n   358→```\n   359→X-RateLimit-Limit: 600\n   360→X-RateLimit-Remaining: 587\n   361→X-RateLimit-Reset: 1714928420\n   362→```\n   363→\n   364→Exceeding returns `429 Too Many Requests` with a `Retry-After` header.\n   365→\n   366→---\n   367→\n   368→## 9. Observability & Tracing\n   369→All requests must include a UUID4 `X-Correlation-Id` header; otherwise the gateway assigns one.\n   370→\n   371→Structured logs are emitted in the [OpenTelemetry](https://opentelemetry.io/) semantic conventions and shipped to the **LogDNA** cluster.  \n   372→Tracing: W3C `traceparent` header is honored.\n   373→\n   374→---\n   375→\n   376→## 10. Getting Started\nBase URL (production):\n\n```\nhttps://api.ledgerlink.io\n```\n\nSandbox (free tier):\n\n```\nhttps://sandbox.ledgerlink.io\n```\n\nAll requests **must** be served over HTTPS. HTTP requests return `426 Upgrade Required`.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Versioning Strategy\nLedgerLink Nexus follows a _media-type driven_ versioning model.\n\n```\nAccept: application/vnd.ledgerlink.v1+json\n```\n\nMajor version bumps are announced 90 days in advance via status page and in the `X-Deprecation` header.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Authentication & Authorization\nNexus supports two authentication flows:\n\n1. OAuth 2.1 Client Credentials (recommended)  \n2. Long-lived Service Tokens (legacy)\n\nInclude the token in the `Authorization` header:\n\n```\nAuthorization: Bearer <access_token>\n```\n\nScopes map one-to-one with CQRS command/query capabilities:\n\n| Scope                   | Grants                                       |\n|-------------------------|----------------------------------------------|\n| `ledgers:read`          | List / fetch ledger entries                  |\n| `ledgers:write`         | Create / update ledger entries               |\n| `accounts:read`         | Account snapshots & cash-flow forecasts      |\n| `webhooks:manage`       | Register, pause, delete webhooks             |\n\nA token with insufficient scopes returns:\n\n```\nHTTP/1.1 403 Forbidden\nLedger-Error-Code: AUTH_SCOPE_INSUFFICIENT\n```\n\n---\n\n## 4. Error Handling Contract\nLedgerLink uses structured JSON error envelopes inspired by RFC 7807.\n\n```json\n{\n  \"error\": {\n    \"code\": \"VALIDATION_FAILED\",\n    \"message\": \"Amount must be greater than zero\",\n    \"details\": [\n      {\n        \"field\": \"amount\",\n        \"issue\": \"must_be_positive\"\n      }\n    ],\n    \"correlation_id\": \"2d501f96-e65b-4b93-a7c8-a7269f30a8ea\",\n    \"timestamp\": \"2024-05-05T08:17:21.155Z\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n| HTTP Status | Code Prefix        | Layer                       |\n|-------------|-------------------|-----------------------------|\n| 4xx         | VALIDATION / AUTH | Request validation, auth    |\n| 5xx         | INTERNAL          | Service / DB / Upstream     |\n\n---\n\n## 5. REST Endpoints\n\n### 5.1 Ledger Entries\n\n#### 5.1.1 List Entries\n```\nGET /v1/ledgers/entries\n```\n\nQuery parameters:\n\n| Name          | Type   | Description                                    |\n|---------------|--------|------------------------------------------------|\n| `account_id`  | UUID   | Filter by account                              |\n| `from_date`   | date   | Inclusive start (ISO-8601)                     |\n| `to_date`     | date   | Inclusive end (ISO-8601)                       |\n| `page[size]`  | int    | Items per page (default: 100, max: 1000)       |\n| `page[cursor]`| string | Cursor returned by previous call               |\n\nExample:\n\n```bash\ncurl -H \"Accept: application/vnd.ledgerlink.v1+json\" \\\n     -H \"Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN\" \\\n     \"https://api.ledgerlink.io/v1/ledgers/entries?account_id=ef83914f-4b2b-4ea5-9d38-38d79f5e70bc&page[size]=50\"\n```\n\nSuccessful response (`200 OK`):\n\n```json\n{\n  \"data\": [\n    {\n      \"entry_id\": \"2e862563-37f7-4b9e-a55b-5f55fb8028b8\",\n      \"account_id\": \"ef83914f-4b2b-4ea5-9d38-38d79f5e70bc\",\n      \"amount\": 1542.25,\n      \"currency\": \"USD\",\n      \"direction\": \"DEBIT\",\n      \"posting_date\": \"2024-04-30\",\n      \"description\": \"Invoice 45602\",\n      \"external_ref\": \"ERP#45602\",\n      \"created_at\": \"2024-04-30T12:34:42.125Z\",\n      \"updated_at\": \"2024-04-30T12:34:42.125Z\"\n    }\n    // ...\n  ],\n  \"meta\": {\n    \"page\": {\n      \"has_next\": true,\n      \"next_cursor\": \"g3QAAAACZAACaWRtAAAAPGU4\"\n    },\n    \"cache\": {\n      \"etag\": \"W/\\\"67d32bca9\\\"\",\n      \"max_age_seconds\": 30\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nServers MAY return the `ETag` header. Supplying `If-None-Match` will trigger a `304 Not Modified`.\n\n#### 5.1.2 Create Entry\n```\nPOST /v1/ledgers/entries\nContent-Type: application/json\n```\n\n```json\n{\n  \"account_id\": \"ef83914f-4b2b-4ea5-9d38-38d79f5e70bc\",\n  \"amount\": 1542.25,\n  \"currency\": \"USD\",\n  \"direction\": \"DEBIT\",\n  \"posting_date\": \"2024-04-30\",\n  \"description\": \"Invoice 45602\",\n  \"external_ref\": \"ERP#45602\"\n}\n```\n\nValidation rules enforced by the View-Model:\n\n* `amount > 0`\n* `currency` conforms to ISO 4217\n* `posting_date` ≤ current date\n* `external_ref` ≤ 100 characters (UTF-8)\n\nOn success (`201 Created`) the response body matches GET by ID.\n\n#### 5.1.3 Retrieve Entry\n```\nGET /v1/ledgers/entries/{entry_id}\n```\n\nReturns `404 Not Found` when the entry does not exist or tenant mismatch.\n\n#### 5.1.4 Update Entry\n```\nPATCH /v1/ledgers/entries/{entry_id}\nContent-Type: application/merge-patch+json\n```\n\nImmutable fields (`entry_id`, `account_id`, `currency`, `direction`) are ignored with `412 Precondition Failed`.\n\n---\n\n### 5.2 Account Snapshots\n\n```\nGET /v1/accounts/{account_id}/snapshots\n```\n\nSnapshots collapse entries into daily balances.\n\n| Query Param | Description                         |\n|-------------|-------------------------------------|\n| `from_date` | Inclusive start date (ISO-8601)     |\n| `to_date`   | Inclusive end date   (ISO-8601)     |\n\n```json\n{\n  \"data\": [\n    {\n      \"as_of\": \"2024-04-30\",\n      \"balance\": 78542.91,\n      \"currency\": \"USD\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"meta\": {\n    \"generated_at\": \"2024-04-30T23:59:59Z\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\nSnapshots are cached aggressively (TTL = 300s) and revalidated on ledger mutations.\n\n---\n\n### 5.3 Cash-Flow Forecasts\n\n```\nPOST /v1/cashflows/forecasts\n```\n\nRequest body:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"account_ids\": [\"ef83914f-4b2b-4ea5-9d38-38d79f5e70bc\"],\n  \"horizon_days\": 90,\n  \"scenario\": \"BASELINE\"\n}\n```\n\nResponse (`202 Accepted`) returns a `forecast_id` and begins async processing.  \nPoll status:\n\n```\nGET /v1/cashflows/forecasts/{forecast_id}\n```\n\nPossible `status` values: `PENDING`, `IN_PROGRESS`, `READY`, `FAILED`.\n\n---\n\n## 6. GraphQL Endpoint\n\n```\nPOST /graphql\nContent-Type: application/json\n```\n\n### 6.1 Schema Snapshot (excerpt)\n\n```graphql\ntype LedgerEntry {\n  entryId: UUID!\n  accountId: UUID!\n  amount: Decimal!\n  currency: Currency!\n  direction: Direction!\n  postingDate: Date!\n  description: String\n  externalRef: String\n  createdAt: DateTime!\n  updatedAt: DateTime!\n}\n\ntype Query {\n  ledgerEntries(\n    accountId: UUID\n    fromDate: Date\n    toDate: Date\n    after: String\n    first: Int = 100\n  ): LedgerEntryConnection!\n}\n\ntype Mutation {\n  createLedgerEntry(input: CreateLedgerEntryInput!): LedgerEntry!\n  updateLedgerEntry(entryId: UUID!, patch: UpdateLedgerEntryPatch!): LedgerEntry!\n}\n```\n\n### 6.2 Example Query\n\n```graphql\nquery Entries($account: UUID!, $cursor: String) {\n  ledgerEntries(accountId: $account, first: 50, after: $cursor) {\n    nodes {\n      entryId\n      amount\n      currency\n      postingDate\n    }\n    pageInfo {\n      hasNextPage\n      endCursor\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## 7. Pagination & Caching\nNexus employs _cursor-based pagination_ (`page[cursor]`). Cursors are opaque Base64-encoded and signed.\n\nResponses MAY include:\n\n```\nCache-Control: private, max-age=30, stale-while-revalidate=30\nETag: W/\"67d32bca9\"\n```\n\nClients SHOULD provide `If-None-Match` for cache hits and store cursors per filter signature.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Rate Limiting\nDefault production limits:\n\n| Resource            | Limit                       |\n|---------------------|-----------------------------|\n| **Authenticated**   | 600 requests / minute / key |\n| **Unauthenticated** | *Not supported*             |\n\nHeaders:\n\n```\nX-RateLimit-Limit: 600\nX-RateLimit-Remaining: 587\nX-RateLimit-Reset: 1714928420\n```\n\nExceeding returns `429 Too Many Requests` with a `Retry-After` header.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Observability & Tracing\nAll requests must include a UUID4 `X-Correlation-Id` header; otherwise the gateway assigns one.\n\nStructured logs are emitted in the [OpenTelemetry](https://opentelemetry.io/) semantic conventions and shipped to the **LogDNA** cluster.  \nTracing: W3C `traceparent` header is honored.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Changelog\n\n### 1.4.0 – 2024-05-01\n* Added `direction` field enum values validation.\n* Introduced async forecast processing with `/cashflows/forecasts`.\n\n### 1.3.0 – 2024-03-26\n* Experimental GraphQL endpoint (`/graphql`) graduated to GA.\n\n### 1.2.2 – 2024-02-14\n* Improved pagination performance with Redis cursor cache.\n\n---\n\n_For questions, contact dev-support@ledgerlink.io or join our Slack community._  \n```","numLines":391,"startLine":1,"totalLines":391}}}

115→/// Abstraction hiding persistent storage semantics.\n   116→#[async_trait]\n   117→pub trait LedgerEntryRepository: Send + Sync {\n   118→    async fn insert(&self, entry: &LedgerEntry) ->     .await\n   139→            .map_err(LedgerError::Db)?;\n   140→\n   141→        Ok(Self { pool })\n   142→    }\n   143→}\n   144→\n   145→#[async_trait]\n   146→impl LedgerEntryRepository for ($1,        $2,         $3,     $4,       $5,        $6)\n   154→            RETURNING id;\n   155→        \"#,\n   156→        )\n   157→        .bind(entry.tenant_id)\n   158→        .bind(entry.account_id)\n   159→        .bind(entry.amount)\n   160→        .bind(&entry.currency)\n   161→        .bind(entry.booked_at)\n   162→        .bind(&entry.memo)\n   163→        .fetch_one(&self.pool)\n   164→        .await?;\n   165→\n   166→        Ok(rec.get::<i64, _>(\"id\"))\n   167→    }\n   168→\n   169→    #[instrument(skip(self), level = \"trace\")]\n   170→    async Ok(rec)\n   186→    }\n   187→\n   188→    #[instrument(skip(self), level = \"debug\")]\n   189→    async fn fetch_page(\n   190→        &self,\n   191→        tenant_id: i64,\n   192→        req: The caching strategy is a simple “cache-aside”\n   227→/// with a 10 minute TTL. We purposely keep the implementation thin; in real\n   228→/// life we would centralise this logic through a reusable Redis wrapper.\n   229→#[derive(Clone)]\n   230→pub struct LedgerEntryService<R: LedgerEntryRepository> {\n   231→    repo: Arc<R>,\n   232→    redis: redis::Client,\n   233→    // Small in-memory cache to ameliorate thundering herd during short periods.\n   234→    in_memory: Arc<RwLock<lru::LruCache<i64, LedgerEntryDto>>>,\n   235→}\n   236→\n   237→impl<R: LedgerEntryRepository> LedgerEntryService<R> {\n   238→    pub fn new(repo: R, redis_url: &str, mem_cache_cap: usize) -> Result<Self> {\n   239→        Ok(Self {\n   240→            repo: Arc::new(repo),\n   241→            redis: redis::Client::open(redis_url).map_err(LedgerError::Cache)?,\n   242→            in_memory: Arc::new(RwLock::new(lru::LruCache::new(\n   243→                mem_cache_cap.try_into().unwrap_or(256),\n   244→            ))),\n   245→        })\n   246→    }\n   247→\n   248→    fn redis_key(tenant_id: i64, entry_id: i64) -> String {\n   249→        format!(\"tenant:{tenant_id}:ledger_entry:{entry_id}\")\n   250→    }\n   251→\n   252→    /// Inserts a new entry and invalidates affected caches.\n   253→    #[instrument(skip(self, entry), level = \"info\")]\n   254→    pub async fn create_entry(&self, entry: &LedgerEntry) -> Result<i64> {\n   255→        // Basic validation rule: amount cannot be 0.\n   256→        if entry.amount.is_zero() {\n   257→            return Err(LedgerError::InvalidState(\n   258→                \"Amount cannot be zero\".to_string(),\n   259→            ));\n   260→        }\n   261→\n   262→        let id = self.repo.insert(entry).await?;\n   263→\n   264→        // Invalidate parent list (simple strategy: delete entire key space).\n   265→        let mut conn = self.redis.get_async_connection().await?;\n   266→        let pattern = format!(\"tenant:{}:ledger_entry:*\", entry.tenant_id);\n   267→        let _: () = redis::cmd(\"UNLINK\").arg(pattern).query_async(&mut conn).await?;\n   268→\n   269→        // Evict in-memory cache.\n   270→        self.in_memory.write().await.pop(&id);\n   271→\n   272→        Ok(id)\n   273→    }\n   274→\n   275→    /// Fetches a single entry, going through L1 (memory) & L2 (Redis) caches.\n   276→    #[instrument(skip(self), level = \"debug\")]\n   277→    pub async fn get_entry(\n   278→        &self,\n   279→        tenant_id: i64,\n   280→        entry_id: i64,\n   281→    ) -> Result<LedgerEntryDto> {\n   282→        // 1. Check L1.\n   283→        if let Some(dto) = self.in_memory.read().await.get(&entry_id).cloned() {\n   284→            return Ok(dto);\n   285→        }\n   286→\n   287→        // 2. Check Redis.\n   288→        let redis_key = Self::redis_key(tenant_id, entry_id);\n   289→        let mut conn = self.redis.get_async_connection().await?;\n   290→        if let Ok(Some(binary)) = conn.get::<_, Vec<u8>>(&redis_key).await {\n   291→            let dto: LedgerEntryDto = bincode::deserialize(&binary)\n   292→                .map_err(|e| LedgerError::InvalidState(e.to_string()))?;\n   293→            // Fill L1.\n   294→            self.in_memory.write().await.put(entry_id, dto.clone());\n   295→            return Ok(dto);\n   296→        }\n   297→\n   298→        // 3. LedgerEntry repository + service layer implementation.\n//!\n//! This module wires together persistence (PostgreSQL), transient caching\n//! IMPORTANT: this file is placed under `src/module_67.txt` only to satisfy\n//! the prompt. In a real code-base it would live in `src/ledger_entry.rs`.\n\nuse std::sync::Arc;\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\nuse redis::AsyncCommands;\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse sqlx::{postgres::PgPoolOptions, PgPool, Row};\nuse thiserror::Error;\nuse tokio::sync::RwLock;\nuse tracing::{instrument, Instrument};\n\n/// Max number of records the API will ever return in a single call.\n/// Acts as a safeguard against pathological requests.\nconst HARD_PAGE_SIZE_LIMIT: u16 = 1_000;\n\n/// Domain object representing an immutable entry in the ledger.\n///\n/// NOTE: The table uses `NUMERIC(38, 10)` for `amount` in Postgres but we\n/// materialise it as `rust_decimal::Decimal` to maintain precision.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct LedgerEntry {\n    pub id: i64,\n    pub tenant_id: i64,\n    pub account_id: i64,\n    pub amount: rust_decimal::Decimal,\n    pub currency: String,\n    pub booked_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub LedgerEntryDto {\n    pub uuid: String,\n    pub amount: rust_decimal::Decimal,\n    pub currency: String,\n    pub booked_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub memo: Option<String>,\n}\n\nimpl From<LedgerEntry> for LedgerEntryDto {\n    fn from(e: LedgerEntry) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            uuid: base62::encode(e.id as u128),\n            amount: e.amount,\n            currency: e.currency,\n            booked_at: e.booked_at,\n            memo: e.memo,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Pagination request coming from the API.\n///\n/// `page` is 1-based because it is more ergonomic for clients.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct PageRequest {\n    pub page: u32,\n    pub per_page: u16,\n}\n\nimpl PageRequest {\n    /// Sanitises caller input: caps `per_page` and guarantees `page >= 1`.\n    pub fn sanitise(self) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            page: self.page.max(1),\n            per_page: self.per_page.min(HARD_PAGE_SIZE_LIMIT).max(1),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Calculates SQL offset.\n    pub fn offset(&self) -> i64 {\n        ((self.page - 1) * self.per_page as u32) as i64\n    }\n}\n\n/// Structured page response returned to the API layer.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct Page<T> {\n    pub data: Vec<T>,\n    pub next_page: Option<u32>,\n    pub sqlx::Error),\n\n    #[error(\"redis error: {0}\")]\n    Cache(#[from] redis::RedisError),\n\n    #[error(\"ledger entry not found\")]\n    NotFound,\n\n    #[error(\"invalid state: {0}\")]\n    InvalidState(String),\n}\n\npub type Result<T> = std::result::Result<T, LedgerError>;\n\n/// Abstraction hiding persistent storage semantics.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait LedgerEntryRepository: Send + Sync {\n    async fn insert(&self, entry: &LedgerEntry) -> Result<i64>;\n    async fn get(&self, tenant_id: i64, entry_id: i64) -> Result<LedgerEntry>;\n    async fn fetch_page(\n        &self,\n module.\npub struct PostgresLedgerEntryRepo {\n    pool: PgPool,\n}\n\nimpl PostgresLedgerEntryRepo {\n    pub async fn new(database_url: &str, max_connections: u32) -> Result<Self> {\n        let })\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl LedgerEntryRepository for PostgresLedgerEntryRepo {\n    #[instrument(skip(self, entry), level = \"debug\")]\n    async fn insert(&self, entry: &LedgerEntry) -> Result<i64> {\n        let rec = sqlx::query(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO ledger_entries\n                    (tenant_id, account_id, amount, currency, booked_at, memo)\n            VALUES  ($1,        $2,         $3,     $4,       $5,        $6)\n            RETURNING id;\n        \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(entry.tenant_id)\n        .bind(entry.account_id)\n        .bind(entry.amount)\n        .bind(&entry.currency)\n        .bind(entry.booked_at)\n        .bind(&entry.memo)\n        .fetch_one(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        Ok(rec.get::<i64, _>(\"id\"))\n    }\n\n    #[instrument(skip(self), level = \"trace\")]\n    async fn get(&self, tenant_id: i64, entry_id: i64) -> Result<LedgerEntry> {\n        let rec = sqlx::query_as!(\n            LedgerEntry,\n            r#\"\n            SELECT id, tenant_id, account_id, amount, currency, booked_at, memo\n            FROM   ledger_entries\n            WHERE  tenant_id = $1 AND id = $2\n        \"#,\n            tenant_id,\n            entry_id\n        )\n        .fetch_optional(&self.pool)\n        .await?\n        .ok_or(LedgerError::NotFound)?;\n\n        Ok(rec)\n    }\n\n    #[instrument(skip(self), level = \"debug\")]\n    async fn fetch_page(\n        &self,\n        tenant_id: i64,\n        req: PageRequest,\n    ) -> Result<(Vec<LedgerEntry>, u64)> {\n        // 1. Count total for the tenant.\n        let total: (i64,) = sqlx::query_as(\n            r#\"SELECT COUNT(*) as count FROM ledger_entries WHERE tenant_id = $1\"#,\n        )\n        .bind(tenant_id)\n        .fetch_one(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        // 2. Fetch requested slice.\n        let rows = sqlx::query_as!(\n            LedgerEntry,\n            r#\"\n            SELECT id, tenant_id, account_id, amount, currency, booked_at, memo\n            FROM   ledger_entries\n            WHERE  tenant_id = $1\n            ORDER  BY booked_at DESC, id DESC\n            OFFSET $2 LIMIT $3\n        \"#,\n            tenant_id,\n            req.offset(),\n            req.per_page as i64\n        )\n        .fetch_all(&self.pool)\n        .await?;\n\n        Ok((rows, total.0 as u64))\n    }\n}\n\n/// High-level service orchestrating repository and distributed cache.\n///\n/// Caches individual `LedgerEntryDto` objects to minimise DB traffic for\n/// idempotent GET requests. The caching strategy is a simple “cache-aside”\n/// with a 10 minute TTL. We purposely keep the implementation thin; in real\n/// life we would centralise this logic through a reusable Redis wrapper.\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct LedgerEntryService<R: LedgerEntryRepository> {\n    repo: Arc<R>,\n    redis: redis::Client,\n    // Small in-memory cache to ameliorate thundering herd during short periods.\n    in_memory: Arc<RwLock<lru::LruCache<i64, LedgerEntryDto>>>,\n}\n\nimpl<R: LedgerEntryRepository> LedgerEntryService<R> {\n    pub fn new(repo: R, redis_url: &str, mem_cache_cap: usize) -> Result<Self> {\n        Ok(Self {\n            repo: Arc::new(repo),\n            redis: redis::Client::open(redis_url).map_err(LedgerError::Cache)?,\n            in_memory: Arc::new(RwLock::new(lru::LruCache::new(\n                mem_cache_cap.try_into().unwrap_or(256),\n            ))),\n        })\n    }\n\n    fn redis_key(tenant_id: i64, entry_id: i64) -> String {\n        format!(\"tenant:{tenant_id}:ledger_entry:{entry_id}\")\n    }\n\n    /// Inserts a new entry and invalidates affected caches.\n    #[instrument(skip(self, entry), level = \"info\")]\n    pub async fn create_entry(&self, entry: &LedgerEntry) -> Result<i64> {\n        // Basic validation rule: amount cannot be 0.\n        if entry.amount.is_zero() {\n            return Err(LedgerError::InvalidState(\n                \"Amount cannot be zero\".to_string(),\n            ));\n        }\n\n        let id = self.repo.insert(entry).await?;\n\n        // Invalidate parent list (simple strategy: delete entire key space).\n        let mut conn = self.redis.get_async_connection().await?;\n        let pattern = format!(\"tenant:{}:ledger_entry:*\", entry.tenant_id);\n        let _: () = redis::cmd(\"UNLINK\").arg(pattern).query_async(&mut conn).await?;\n\n        // Evict in-memory cache.\n        self.in_memory.write().await.pop(&id);\n\n        Ok(id)\n    }\n\n    /// Fetches a single entry, going through L1 (memory) & L2 (Redis) caches.\n    #[instrument(skip(self), level = \"debug\")]\n    pub async fn get_entry(\n        &self,\n        tenant_id: i64,\n        entry_id: i64,\n    ) -> Result<LedgerEntryDto> {\n        // 1. Check L1.\n        if let Some(dto) = self.in_memory.read().await.get(&entry_id).cloned() {\n            return Ok(dto);\n        }\n\n        // 2. Check Redis.\n        let redis_key = Self::redis_key(tenant_id, entry_id);\n        let mut conn = self.redis.get_async_connection().await?;\n        if let Ok(Some(binary)) = conn.get::<_, Vec<u8>>(&redis_key).await {\n            let dto: LedgerEntryDto = bincode::deserialize(&binary)\n                .map_err(|e| LedgerError::InvalidState(e.to_string()))?;\n            // Fill L1.\n            self.in_memory.write().await.put(entry_id, dto.clone());\n            return Ok(dto);\n        }\n\n        // 3. Fallback to DB.\n        let entry = self.repo.get(tenant_id, entry_id).await?;\n        let dto = LedgerEntryDto::from(entry.clone());\n\n        // Back-fill caches.\n        let _: () = conn\n            .set_ex(\n                &redis_key,\n                bincode::serialize(&dto).unwrap(),\n                600, // seconds\n            )\n            .await?;\n        self.in_memory.write().await.put(entry_id, dto.clone());\n\n        Ok(dto)\n    }\n\n    /// Retrieves a paginated list, with optional total count.\n    #[instrument(skip(self), level = \"debug\")]\n    pub async fn list_entries(\n        &self,\n        tenant_id: i64,\n        req: PageRequest,\n    ) -> Result<Page<LedgerEntryDto>> {\n        let req = req.sanitise();\n        // At scale we would paginated-cache “pages” too, but omit for brevity.\n        let (rows, total) = self.repo.fetch_page(tenant_id, req).await?;\n        let data: Vec<LedgerEntryDto> = rows.into_iter().map(Into::into).collect();\n\n        let consumed = (req.page as u64) * (req.per_page as u64);\n        let next_page = if consumed < total {\n            Some(req.page + 1)\n        } else {\n            None\n        };\n\n        Ok(Page {\n            data,\n            next_page,\n            total: Some(total),\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use rust_decimal_macros::dec;\n    use serial_test::serial;\n\n    // Helper to build an in-memory postgres (requires `cargo test -- --ignored`\n    // if dockerised). Kept minimal for brevity.\n    async fn bootstrap_repo() -> PostgresLedgerEntryRepo {\n        let db_url =\n            std::env::var(\"TEST_DATABASE_URL\").expect(\"Set TEST_DATABASE_URL\");\n        let repo = PostgresLedgerEntryRepo::new(&db_url, 5)\n            .await\n            .expect(\"failed to init repo\");\n\n        // Initialise schema (idempotent).\n        sqlx::migrate!().run(&repo.pool).await.unwrap();\n\n        repo\n    }\n\n    #[tokio::test]\n    #[serial] // Avoid test inter-dependency on the DB.\n    async fn crud_roundtrip() {\n        let repo = bootstrap_repo().await;\n        let svc =\n            LedgerEntryService::new(repo, \"redis://127.0.0.1/\", 128).unwrap();\n\n        let entry = LedgerEntry {\n            id: 0,\n            tenant_id: 42,\n            account_id: 100,\n            amount: dec!(123.45),\n            currency: \"USD\".into(),\n            booked_at: Utc::now(),\n            memo: Some(\"integration test\".into()),\n        };\n\n        let id = svc.create_entry(&entry).await.unwrap();\n        assert!(id > 0);\n\n        let dto1 = svc.get_entry(42, id).await.unwrap();\n        let dto2 = svc.get_entry(42, id).await.unwrap(); // should hit cache\n\n        assert_eq!(dto1.amount, dto2.amount);\n\n        let page = svc\n            .list_entries(\n                42,\n                PageRequest {\n                    page: 1,\n                    per_page: 10,\n                },\n            )\n            .await\n            .unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(page.data.len(), 1);\n    }\n}\n```","numLines":401,"startLine":1,"totalLines":401}}}

High-level responsibilities\n//! ---------------------------\n//! 1. Parse & validate incoming pagination parameters\n//! 2. Offer a *view-model* friendly `Paginated<T>` envelope\n//! 3. validate_offset(offset: &u32) -> Result<(), ValidationError> {\n    if *offset > 100_000 {\n        return Err(ValidationError::new(\"offset_too_large\"));\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Custom limit .get(\"offset\")\n            .and_then(|v| v.parse::<u32>().ok())\n            .unwrap_or_default();\n\n        let limit = params\n            .get(\"limit\")\n            .and_then(|v| Pagination-aware, type-safe Redis cache for “Query” side of the\n//! Command/Query separation used by LedgerLink Nexus.  The cache is designed\n//! to work in a multi-tenant, horizontally-scaled micro-service and therefore\n//! supports:\n//!   • namespaced keys (tenant isolation)\n//!   • expirations / soft TTL\n//!   • pub/sub-based fan-out invalidation\n//!   • structured tracing and metrics\n//!\n//! The public API intentionally mimics an ordinary repository call so the\n//! surrounding service layer can stay oblivious to the actual caching\n//! strategy.\n//!\n//! # Example\n//! ```no_run\n//! # use uuid::Uuid;\n//! # use ledgerlink_nexus::cache::{PagedCache, Pagination, CacheableQuery};\n//! # use tokio::runtime::Runtime;\n//! #\n//! #[derive(Clone, Debug)]\n//! struct GetLedgerEntryPage {\n//!     pub tenant_id: Uuid,\n//!     pub account_id: Uuid,\n//!     pub pagination: Pagination,\n//! }\n//!\n//! impl CacheableQuery for GetLedgerEntryPage {\n//!     fn tenant_id(&self) -> Uuid { self.tenant_id }\n//!     fn pagination(&self) -> &Pagination { &self.pagination }\n//!     fn cache_hash(&self) -> String {\n//!         format!(\"account:{}\", self.account_id)\n//!     }\n//! }\n//!\n//! # Runtime::new().unwrap().block_on(async move {\n//! let redis_url = \"redis://127.0.0.1/\";\n//! let cache = PagedCache::<serde_json::Value>::connect(redis_url)\n//!     .await\n//!     .expect(\"redis must be up\");\n//!\n//! let query = GetLedgerEntryPage {\n//!     tenant_id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n//!     account_id: Uuid::new_v4(),\n//!     pagination: Pagination::new(0, 50).unwrap(),\n//! };\n//!\n//! let (_v, from_cache) = cache\n//!     .get_or_query(&query, || async {\n//!         // .. expensive DB call here ..\n//!         Ok(serde_json::json!({\"dummy\": true }))\n//!     })\n//!     .await\n//!     .unwrap();\n//! # });\n//! ```\n\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse bytes::Bytes;\nuse redis::{aio::ConnectionManager, AsyncCommands};\nuse serde::{de::DeserializeOwned, Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse thiserror::Error;\nuse tokio::{sync::broadcast, time};\nuse tracing::{debug, instrument, warn};\nuse uuid::Uuid;\n\n/// Hard upper-bound for `per_page` to protect Redis payload size and memory\n/// pressure.  A single cached page must never exceed this many items.\nconst MAX_PAGE_SIZE: usize = 250;\n\n/// Built-in soft time-to-live for cached pages.\n///\n/// Note: domain services may override this by providing\n/// `PagedCache::with_ttl`.\nconst DEFAULT_TTL: Duration = Duration::from_secs(30);\n\n/// Redis channel used for fan-out invalidation events.\nconst INVALIDATION_CHANNEL: &str = \"ledgerlink:nexus:cache:evict\";\n\n/// Common error envelope returned by this module.\n#[derive(Error, Debug)]\npub enum CacheError {\n    #[error(\"redis error: {0}\")]\n    Redis(#[from] redis::RedisError),\n    #[error(\"serialization error: {0}\")]\n    Bincode(#[from] bincode::Error),\n    #[error(\"application error: {0}\")]\n    Application(#[from] anyhow::Error),\n}\n\n/// Pagination information sent by a caller or deserialized from a request\n/// payload.  It purposely mirrors the GraphQL “Connection” spec as well as the\n/// REST flavour used in our OpenAPI contract.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct Pagination {\n    pub page: usize,\n    pub per_page: usize,\n}\n\nimpl Pagination {\n    /// Construct a new pagination config while enforcing invariants.\n    pub fn new(page: usize, per_page: usize) -> Result<Self, CacheError> {\n        if per_page == 0 || per_page > MAX_PAGE_SIZE {\n            anyhow::bail!(\n                \"per_page must be within 1..={MAX_PAGE_SIZE} (got {per_page})\"\n            );\n        }\n        Ok(Self { page, per_page })\n    }\n}\n\n/// Trait implemented by every Query object that wishes to leverage the cache.\n/// Only a handful of methods are required so the domain code remains\n/// decoupled from Redis.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait CacheableQuery: Send + Sync {\n    /// A tenant-scoped uuid allowing sharding and event fan-out.\n    fn tenant_id(&self) -> Uuid;\n\n    /// Pagination info (page number + page size)\n    fn pagination(&self) -> &Pagination;\n\n    /// Deterministic hash that uniquely identifies the query parameters\n    /// **excluding** pagination (e.g. account, date_range, filters).\n    ///\n    /// Think of this as a stable, human-readable identifier.\n    fn cache_hash(&self) -> String;\n}\n\n/// Envelope stored in Redis.  We prepend a short version tag so we can bump\n/// serialization format without flushing the whole store.\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\nstruct CachedEnvelope<T> {\n    v: u8,\n    data: T,\n}\n\n/// A concrete Redis-backed cache aware of pagination and invalidation events.\npub struct PagedCache<T> {\n    conn: ConnectionManager,\n    ttl: Duration,\n    /// Broadcast channel used locally for best-effort, in-process fan-out.\n    evict_tx: broadcast::Sender<String>,\n    _phantom: std::marker::PhantomData<T>,\n}\n\nimpl<T> PagedCache<T>\nwhere\n    T: Serialize + DeserializeOwned + Send + Sync + 'static,\n{\n    /// Connect to Redis and spawn a small background task listening for\n    /// invalidation events.\n    pub async fn connect(redis_url: &str) -> Result<Self, CacheError> {\n        let client = redis::Client::open(redis_url)?;\n        let manager = ConnectionManager::new(client).await?;\n        let (tx, _rx) = broadcast::channel(64);\n\n        // Spawn invalidation listener\n        Self::spawn_invalidator(redis_url.to_owned(), tx.clone());\n\n        Ok(Self {\n            conn: manager,\n            ttl: DEFAULT_TTL,\n            evict_tx: tx,\n            _phantom: std::marker::PhantomData,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Provide a custom TTL.  Fluent-style builder.\n    pub fn with_ttl(mut self, ttl: Duration) -> Self {\n        self.ttl = ttl;\n        self\n    }\n\n    /// Try to fetch from cache or execute `fetch_fn` and then cache the\n    /// outcome.  The second value in the tuple indicates whether it was\n    /// a cache hit.\n    #[instrument(skip_all, fields(cache.key = %self.derive_key(query)))]\n    pub async fn get_or_query<Q, F, E>(\n        &self,\n        query: &Q,\n        fetch_fn: F,\n    ) -> Result<(T, bool), CacheError>\n    where\n        Q: CacheableQuery,\n        F: std::future::Future<Output = Result<T, E>> + Send,\n        E: Into<anyhow::Error> + Send,\n    {\n        let redis_key = self.derive_key(query);\n        // 1. Optimistic local in-process check first\n        if let Ok(value) = self.try_get_local(&redis_key).await {\n            return Ok((value, true));\n        }\n\n        // 2. Grab from Redis\n        let mut conn = self.conn.clone();\n        if let Some(raw): Option<Vec<u8>> = conn.get(&redis_key).await? {\n            let envelope: CachedEnvelope<T> = bincode::deserialize(&raw)?;\n            // No versioning strategy yet, v=0\n            debug!(\"Cache hit (redis)\");\n            self.populate_local(redis_key.clone(), envelope.data.clone())\n                .await;\n            return Ok((envelope.data, true));\n        }\n\n        // 3. Miss – execute provided closure\n        debug!(\"Cache miss; delegating to fetch_fn\");\n        let fresh = fetch_fn.await.map_err(Into::into)?;\n\n        // 4. Save to Redis (fire-and-forget)\n        let save_redis_key = redis_key.clone();\n        let save_ttl = self.ttl;\n        let save_value = fresh.clone();\n        tokio::spawn(async move {\n            let env = CachedEnvelope { v: 0, data: save_value };\n            if let Ok(payload) = bincode::serialize(&env) {\n                let _: Result<(), _> = conn\n                    .set_ex(save_redis_key, payload, save_ttl.as_secs() as usize)\n                    .await;\n            }\n        });\n\n        // 5. Fill local cache\n        self.populate_local(redis_key, fresh.clone()).await;\n        Ok((fresh, false))\n    }\n\n    /// Explicitly evict a query result (all pages) for the given tenant/query\n    /// hash, broadcasting the event to every service instance.\n    ///\n    /// Should be called by the *Command* side whenever a mutation might\n    /// invalidate read models (e.g. posting a new ledger entry).\n    pub async fn evict<Q>(&self, query: &Q) -> Result<(), CacheError>\n    where\n        Q: CacheableQuery,\n    {\n        let pattern = format!(\"nexus:{}:{}:*\", query.tenant_id(), query.cache_hash());\n        let mut conn = self.conn.clone();\n        let _: () = redis::cmd(\"UNLINK\").arg(pattern.clone()).query_async(&mut conn).await?;\n\n        // Broadcast\n        let _ = conn.publish::<_, _, ()>(INVALIDATION_CHANNEL, pattern.clone()).await;\n        // Local channel\n        let _ = self.evict_tx.send(pattern);\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    /// Assemble the final Redis key\n    fn derive_key<Q: CacheableQuery>(&self, query: &Q) -> String {\n        format!(\n            \"nexus:{}:{}:{}:{}\",\n            query.tenant_id(),\n            query.cache_hash(),\n            query.pagination().page,\n            query.pagination().per_page\n        )\n    }\n\n    // ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n    // Local in-process cache (tiny, bounded, non-blocking)\n    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    async fn try_get_local(&self, k: &str) -> Result<T, CacheError> {\n        use moka::future::Cache;\n        static LOCAL: once_cell::sync::Lazy<Cache<String, Bytes>> =\n            once_cell::sync::Lazy::new(|| Cache::builder()\n                .max_capacity(4_096)\n                .time_to_live(DEFAULT_TTL)\n                .build());\n\n        if let Some(bytes) = LOCAL.get(k).await {\n            let env: CachedEnvelope<T> = bincode::deserialize(bytes.as_ref())?;\n            return Ok(env.data);\n        }\n        anyhow::bail!(\"not in local cache\")\n    }\n\n    async fn populate_local(&self, k: String, data: T) {\n        use moka::future::Cache;\n        static LOCAL: once_cell::sync::Lazy<Cache<String, Bytes>> =\n            once_cell::sync::Lazy::new(|| Cache::builder()\n                .max_capacity(4_096)\n                .time_to_live(DEFAULT_TTL)\n                .build());\n\n        if let Ok(payload) = bincode::serialize(&CachedEnvelope { v: 0, data }) {\n            let _ = LOCAL.insert(k, Bytes::from(payload)).await;\n        }\n    }\n\n    // ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n    // Background Redis pub/sub listener – distribute invalidation events\n    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    fn spawn_invalidator(redis_url: String, evict_tx: broadcast::Sender<String>) {\n        tokio::spawn(async move {\n            loop {\n                match redis::Client::open(redis_url.as_str())\n                    .and_then(|c| c.get_async_connection())\n                    .await\n                {\n                    Ok(conn) => {\n                        if let Err(e) = Self::listen_invalidation(conn, evict_tx.clone()).await {\n                            warn!(error = %e, \"cache invalidation listener died; restarting\");\n                        };\n                    }\n                    Err(e) => {\n                        warn!(error = %e, \"cannot connect to redis for invalidation; retrying\");\n                        time::sleep(Duration::from_secs(5)).await;\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        });\n    }\n\n    async fn listen_invalidation(\n        mut conn: redis::aio::Connection,\n        evict_tx: broadcast::Sender<String>,\n    ) -> Result<(), CacheError> {\n        use redis::AsyncCommands;\n\n        let mut pubsub = conn.as_pubsub();\n        pubsub.subscribe(INVALIDATION_CHANNEL).await?;\n\n        loop {\n            let msg = pubsub.on_message().await;\n            let payload: String = msg.get_payload()?;\n            // Re-emit into local broadcast\n            let _ = evict_tx.send(payload.clone());\n\n            // Purge local moka cache entries matching the pattern\n            Self::purge_local_matching(&payload).await;\n        }\n    }\n\n    async fn purge_local_matching(pattern: &str) {\n        use moka::future::Cache;\n        static LOCAL: once_cell::sync::Lazy<Cache<String, Bytes>> =\n            once_cell::sync::Lazy::new(|| Cache::builder()\n                .max_capacity(4_096)\n                .time_to_live(DEFAULT_TTL)\n                .build());\n\n        let keys: Vec<String> = LOCAL.iter().map(|(k, _)| k.clone()).collect();\n        for k in keys {\n            if k.starts_with(pattern) {\n                LOCAL.invalidate(&k).await;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n```","numLines":357,"startLine":1,"totalLines":357}}}

This causes clients to receive stale data until the cache entry's TTL expires naturally.\n\n## Key Files Identified\n\n### Core Caching Modules\n- `/app/project/src/module_44.txt` - **Primary caching module**: High-volume Ledger Entry listing with Redis response-level caching. Contains `LedgerEntryQuerySvc` with `try_fetch_cache()` and `persist_cache()` methods, but only READ operations interact with cache.\n- `/app/project/src/module_46.txt` - **Pagination-aware Redis cache**: Contains `PagedCache<T>` with an explicit `evict()` method that *should* be called by write handlers but isn't wired to them.\n- `/app/project/src/module_33.txt` - **ResponseCache trait**: Defines cache abstraction with `invalidate()` method but write handlers don't use it.\n\n### Read Handler Modules (Query Side)\n- `/app/project/src/module_26.txt` - Ledger Entry Query Endpoint: Implements `get_ledger_entries()` handler that checks Redis cache before querying the database, stores results in cache on miss.\n- `/app/project/src/module_52.txt` - High-frequency paginated read-model for ledger entries with `LedgerEntryService::get_paginated()` that uses cache.\n- `/app/project/src/module_71.txt` - Cash-Flow Forecast query service with Redis-backed response caching.\n\n### Write Handler Modules (Command Side)\n- `/app/project/src/module_67.txt` - **Contains BOTH read and write operations**: Has `create_entry()` which properly invalidates cache (the exception), but this pattern is not replicated across other modules.\n- `/app/project/src/module_38.txt` - `LedgerEntryPgRepository::insert()` - Writes to database but has **NO cache invalidation**.\n\n### Configuration\n- `/app/project/src/config.txt` - Contains `ResponseCacheConfig` with `duration: Duration` (default 5 minutes TTL).\n- `/app/project/package.json` - Project manifest showing Rust-based microservice.\n- `/app/project/docs/api.md` - API documentation defining REST and GraphQL mutations.\n\n## Code Evidence\n\n### Evidence 1: Read Handler with Caching (module_44.txt)\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_44.txt\n// Lines: 355-408\n#[instrument(skip(self))]\npub async fn list(\n    &self,\n    tenant: Uuid,\n    query: ListQuery,\n) -> Result<Paginated<LedgerEntryDto>, ServiceError> {\n    // 1. Try cache (30ms timeout to avoid head-of-line blocking)\n    let key = Self::cache_key(tenant, &query);\n    if let Ok(Some(raw)) = self.try_fetch_cache(&key).await {\n        debug!(%tenant, %key, \"Cache hit\");\n        return serde_json::from_slice::<Paginated<LedgerEntryDto>>(&raw)\n            .map_err(ServiceError::CacheSerialization);\n    }\n\n    // 2. Query repository\n    debug!(%tenant, \"Cache miss – querying DB\");\n    let (entries, total) = self.repo.list(tenant, &query).await?;\n    // ... transforms entries ...\n\n    // 3. Backfill cache (best-effort)\n    let _ = self.persist_cache(&key, &payload).await;\n\n    Ok(payload)\n}\n\nasync fn persist_cache<T: Serialize>(&self, key: &str, value: &T) -> Result<(), ServiceError> {\n    let mut conn: RedisConn = self.redis.get().await?;\n    let data = serde_json::to_vec(value)?;\n    let _: () = conn\n        .set_ex(key, data, 60) // 60 seconds TTL\n        .await\n        .map_err(ServiceError::Cache)?;\n    Ok(())\n}\n```\n\n### Evidence 2: Write Handler WITHOUT Cache Invalidation (module_38.txt)\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_38.txt\n// Lines: 277-306\n/// An *idempotent* command that inserts a ledger entry inside its own\n/// transaction.  Exposed as a convenience; not part of the trait.\n///\n/// Demonstrates how we keep read & write paths separated (`CQRS` style).\nimpl LedgerEntryPgRepository {\n    #[instrument(skip_all, name = \"repo.ledger.insert\")]\n    pub async fn insert(&self, entry: &LedgerEntry) -> Result<(), PaginationError> {\n        let mut tx = self.pool.begin().await?;\n\n        sqlx::query(\n            r#\"\n            INSERT INTO ledger_entries\n            (id, account_id, amount, currency, description, created_at)\n            VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5, $6)\n            ON CONFLICT (id) DO NOTHING\n            \"#,\n        )\n        .bind(entry.id)\n        .bind(entry.account_id)\n        .bind(entry.amount)\n        .bind(&entry.currency)\n        .bind(&entry.description)\n        .bind(entry.created_at)\n        .execute(&mut tx)\n        .await?;\n\n        tx.commit().await?;\n        Ok(())\n        // NOTE: NO cache invalidation here!\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Evidence 3: Proper Cache Invalidation Pattern (module_67.txt)\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_67.txt\n// Lines: 251-272\n/// Inserts a new entry and invalidates affected caches.\n#[instrument(skip(self, entry), level = \"info\")]\npub async fn create_entry(&self, entry: &LedgerEntry) -> Result<i64> {\n    // Basic validation rule: amount cannot be 0.\n    if entry.amount.is_zero() {\n        return Err(LedgerError::InvalidState(\n            \"Amount cannot be zero\".to_string(),\n        ));\n    }\n\n    let id = self.repo.insert(entry).await?;\n\n    // Invalidate parent list (simple strategy: delete entire key space).\n    let mut conn = self.redis.get_async_connection().await?;\n    let pattern = format!(\"tenant:{}:ledger_entry:*\", entry.tenant_id);\n    let _: () = redis::cmd(\"UNLINK\").arg(pattern).query_async(&mut conn).await?;\n\n    // Evict in-memory cache.\n    self.in_memory.write().await.pop(&id);\n\n    Ok(id)\n}\n```\n\n### Evidence 4: Existing Cache Eviction Infrastructure (module_46.txt)\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_46.txt\n// Lines: 232-251\n/// Explicitly evict a query result (all pages) for the given tenant/query\n/// hash, broadcasting the event to every service instance.\n///\n/// Should be called by the *Command* side whenever a mutation might\n/// invalidate read models (e.g. posting a new ledger entry).\npub async fn evict<Q>(&self, query: &Q) -> Result<(), CacheError>\nwhere\n    Q: CacheableQuery,\n{\n    let pattern = format!(\"nexus:{}:{}:*\", query.tenant_id(), query.cache_hash());\n    let mut conn = self.conn.clone();\n    let _: () = redis::cmd(\"UNLINK\").arg(pattern.clone()).query_async(&mut conn).await?;\n\n    // Broadcast\n    let _ = conn.publish::<_, _, ()>(INVALIDATION_CHANNEL, pattern.clone()).await;\n    // Local channel\n    let _ = self.evict_tx.send(pattern);\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n```\n\n### Evidence 5: Cache Configuration (config.txt)\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/config.txt\n// Lines: 197-209\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]\npub struct ResponseCacheConfig {\n    #[serde(default)]\n    pub enabled: bool,\n    #[serde(default = \"default_cache_duration\")]\n    pub duration: Duration,\n    #[serde(default)]\n    pub respect_cache_control_header: bool,\n}\n\nfn default_cache_duration() -> Duration {\n    Duration::from_secs(5 * 60) // 5 minutes\n}\n```\n\n## Analysis\n\n### How Components Interact\n\nThe LedgerLink Nexus system follows a CQRS (Command Query Responsibility Segregation) architecture pattern:\n\n1. **Read Path (Query Side)**:\n   - Client sends GET request → Handler (e.g., `module_44`, `module_26`, `module_52`)\n   - Handler checks Redis cache first using a computed cache key\n   - On cache hit: return cached response immediately\n   - On cache miss: query PostgreSQL repository, cache the result, return to client\n\n2. **Write Path (Command Side)**:\n   - Client sends POST/PUT/DELETE request → Write Handler (e.g., `module_38`, `module_71`)\n   - Handler persists changes to PostgreSQL\n   - **FLAW**: Handler does NOT invalidate or update the Redis cache\n   - Returns success to client\n\n3. **Cache Layer**:\n   - Redis serves as the primary caching backend\n   - Some modules (e.g., `module_67`) also use an in-memory LRU cache as L1\n   - Cache keys are composed of tenant_id + query parameters + pagination\n   - Default TTL is 5 minutes (300 seconds) for most entries, ranging from 15-60 seconds in various modules\n\n### The Architectural Flaw\n\n**The fundamental problem is the absence of an invalidation signal from write handlers to the cache layer.**\n\nWhen a write operation occurs:\n1. The database is updated successfully\n2. The cache still contains the old (stale) data\n3. Subsequent read requests hit the cache and return stale data\n4. Data only becomes consistent when the cache entry's TTL expires\n\nThis is a **classic cache coherence problem** in distributed systems. The codebase has the infrastructure to support cache invalidation (as seen in `module_46` with `PagedCache::evict()` and `module_67` with proper invalidation), but this pattern is not consistently applied across all write handlers.\n\n### Evidence of CQRS Design Intent\n\nThe code comments explicitly state the intent:\n- module_38.txt: \"Demonstrates how we keep read & write paths separated (`CQRS` style)\"\n- module_46.txt: \"Should be called by the *Command* side whenever a mutation might invalidate read models\"\n- module_33.txt: \"The cache is *write-through*: mutations trigger invalidations so we never serve stale financial data\"\n\nHowever, the implementation doesn't fulfill this contract—most write handlers skip the invalidation step.\n\n## Solution Proposal\n\n### Design Pattern: Explicit Cache Invalidation\n\nThe recommended architectural solution is **Explicit Cache Invalidation** (also known as \"Cache-Aside with Manual Eviction\"). This pattern:\n\n1. Write handlers explicitly invalidate relevant cache entries after successful database writes\n2. Uses key pattern matching to invalidate all pages/variations of affected data\n3. Broadcasts invalidation events for distributed cache coherence\n\n### Why This Pattern?\n\n| Alternative | Pros | Cons | Decision |\n|-------------|------|------|----------|\n| **Explicit Invalidation** | Immediate consistency, targeted eviction, already partially implemented | Requires code changes in write handlers | **RECOMMENDED** |\n| Lower TTL | Simple to implement | Doesn't solve problem, just reduces window; hurts performance | NOT RECOMMENDED |\n| Write-through cache | Guarantees consistency | Higher write latency, complex to implement for paginated queries | OVERKILL |\n| Event-driven invalidation | Decoupled, scalable | Adds infrastructure complexity, eventual consistency | FOR FUTURE |\n\n### Justification\n\n1. **Immediate Consistency**: Financial data requires accurate, real-time reporting. Serving stale balances or ledger entries is unacceptable in B2B reconciliation.\n\n2. **Infrastructure Already Exists**: The `PagedCache::evict()` method in module_46 and the pattern in module_67 show the architecture already supports invalidation—it just needs to be wired up.\n\n3. **Targeted Invalidation**: Using key patterns (e.g., `tenant:{id}:ledger_entry:*`) allows efficient bulk invalidation without flushing the entire cache.\n\n4. **Distributed Support**: The pub/sub mechanism in module_46 (`INVALIDATION_CHANNEL`) enables fan-out to all service instances in a horizontally-scaled deployment.\n\n## Implementation Plan\n\n### Modules Requiring Modification\n\n1. **Write Handler Modules** (must ADD cache invalidation calls):\n   - `module_38.txt` (LedgerEntryPgRepository::insert)\n   - `module_71.txt` (CashFlowForecastService - add mutation handlers)\n   - Any other modules with POST/PUT/DELETE handlers for ledger entries\n\n2. **Caching Modules** (may need enhancement):\n   - `module_44.txt` - Add `invalidate_tenant_entries()` method\n   - `module_46.txt` - Already has `evict()`, may need to be exposed more broadly\n   - `module_33.txt` - Ensure `invalidate()` method is accessible\n\n### New Interactions Required\n\n```\n┌─────────────────┐      ┌──────────────────┐      ┌─────────────────┐\n│  Write Handler  │ ───► │  Database (PG)   │      │  Cache (Redis)  │\n│  (module_38)    │      │                  │      │                 │\n└────────┬────────┘      └──────────────────┘      └────────▲────────┘\n         │                                                   │\n         │  2. After successful DB write,                   │\n         │     call cache.invalidate(key_pattern)           │\n         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n                         NEW INTERACTION\n```\n\n### Specific Changes\n\n**module_38.txt - Add cache invalidation to insert():**\n```rust\nimpl LedgerEntryPgRepository {\n    pub async fn insert(\n        &self,\n        entry: &LedgerEntry,\n        cache: &dyn CacheBackend  // NEW: inject cache dependency\n    ) -> Result<(), PaginationError> {\n        // ... existing database insert logic ...\n\n        tx.commit().await?;\n\n        // NEW: Invalidate cache after successful write\n        let pattern = format!(\"ledger_entries:{}:*\", entry.tenant_id);\n        cache.invalidate_pattern(&pattern).await?;\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**module_44.txt - Read operations cache responses in Redis, but write operations (POST/PUT/DELETE) do not invalidate the corresponding cache entries, causing clients to receive outdated data until TTL expiration.\n\n- **Key components**:\n  - Caching: `module_44`, `module_46`, `module_33`\n  - Read handlers: `module_26`, `module_52`, `module_71`\n  - Write handlers: `module_38`, `module_67`\n\n- **Architectural pattern**: The system uses CQRS (Command Query Responsibility Segregation) with a cache-aside pattern. The cache layer exists and has invalidation infrastructure (`PagedCache::evict()`, `ResponseCache::invalidate()`), but write handlers are not wired to call these methods.\n\n- **Root cause**: Write handlers in modules like `module_38` and most mutations do NOT call cache invalidation after successful database writes. The exception is `module_67::create_entry()` which properly invalidates cache—this pattern needs to be replicated across all write handlers.\n\n- **Recommendations**:\n  1. Modify all write handlers (`module_38`, `module_71`, etc.) to call `cache.invalidate()` after successful DB writes\n  2. Use key pattern matching (e.g., `tenant:{id}:*`) for efficient bulk invalidation\n  3. Leverage existing pub/sub infrastructure (`INVALIDATION_CHANNEL`) for distributed cache coherence\n  4.
